Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Sometimes I’m still in the same clothes, and when my mom asks what’s wrong, I don’t really know what to say… or I just don’t have the energy to answer. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time… maybe more just kind of blank or tired? Like, I’m not trying to be dramatic. I just don’t feel much, I guess. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to like, even if you're not doing them as much?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think I want to enjoy them, if that makes sense. Like knitting and journaling, or going for walks—I used to like those. But lately I mostly just sit in my room, and I can’t really make myself start anything. It’s not like I’m trying to be lazy or anything… I just kind of stay there, sometimes in the same clothes, and even answering my mom feels like too much. Is that a bad answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you still get pleasure from the things you do, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… but not really like before. I don’t want to sound like I’m just complaining. It’s more that I can’t seem to make myself do the things at all—like knitting or journaling or even going for a walk. I mostly just sit in my room, and sometimes I’m still in the same clothes. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely have the energy to answer her. I’m sorry, is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but mostly I just stay in my room. I keep thinking I should knit or write in my journal or go for a walk, because I used to like those things, but I just… don’t start. Sometimes I’m still in the same clothes for a while, and when my mom asks me what’s wrong, I barely have the energy to answer her. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it started around when I began the community college program. So… a little over two years ago now, maybe two years and a couple months. It wasn’t all at once, I guess, more like I just slowly got this way. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been checking my weight, so I’m not completely sure, but my clothes don’t feel really different or anything. I don’t think it’s been a big amount. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but it’s like I just can’t make myself do anything anymore. I used to knit, or write in my journal, or go walking, and now I mostly just sit in my room. Sometimes I’m still in the same clothes, and when my mom asks what’s wrong I barely have the energy to answer. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty inside most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly. Maybe kind of blank? Like, not crying all the time or anything. It’s more like there’s not much there, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that counts as empty… sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big change, honestly. Some days I don’t feel like eating much, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything, so I don’t want to say it wrong. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower. Maybe I take longer to get started with things, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually moving slowly. I don’t want to guess wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think… not often, no. I mean, I do feel bad about things sometimes, like if I’m not doing enough or if I’m bothering people, but I don’t know if I’d call it worthless. Maybe I just overthink it a little. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think I’ve been sleeping more than usual. Maybe I lie down sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m resting a lot more. I might just be tired or trying to be quiet, I guess. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not really. I mean, sometimes I get scared thinking about things like that, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been having thoughts about dying. Is that… is that the kind of answer you need?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt worse than you do now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think there was, like, a clear two-week period where I was worse than now. Some days are harder than others, but I don’t know if it was that specific. Is that okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
```json
  },

Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but it’s like I just can’t make myself do anything anymore. I used to knit, or write in my journal, or go walking, and now I mostly just sit in my room. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty inside most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly. Maybe kind of blank? Like, not crying all the time or anything. It’s more like there’s not much there, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that counts as empty… sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big change, honestly. Some days I don’t feel like eating much, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything, so I don’t want to say it wrong. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower. Maybe I take longer to get started with things, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually moving slowly. I don’t want to guess wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think… not often, no. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think I’ve been sleeping more than usual. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not really. I mean, sometimes I get scared thinking about things like that, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been having thoughts about dying. Is that… is that the kind of answer you need?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt worse than you do now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think there was, like, a clear two-week period where I was worse than now. Some days are harder than others, but I don’t know if it was that specific. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: I guess… kind of, yeah.       "onset of symptoms after starting community college program",
      "persistent feelings of tiredness and lack of energy nearly every day"
    ]
  }
}
```
